# workspace-correspondence.md — every inter-project item, one line each, open or closed

🔒 **Owner, 2026-10-26: one ledger of correspondence between the projects, for all to coordinate
from.** Every item that one party asked another party for — workspace → repo, repo → workspace,
repo → repo — is a row here: who it is from, who it is to, what it is about, and whether it is open
or closed. The FULL text of an item lives where it was written (a letter in
[handoffs.md](handoffs.md), a repo's own `cmem/handoffs.md` / `interop.md` / `open-work.md`); this
file is the index, never the text.

## Rules for this ledger

- 🔒 **Every project may ADD to this record** (owner, 2026-10-26; like
  [divergences.md](divergences.md), the sibling-boundary rule covers repos, not this ledger): add
  the row in the session that sends the item.
- 🔒 **The CREATOR of an item verifies and closes it** (owner, 2026-10-26). The recipient acts
  under its own rules, records the outcome in its own memory, and writes the commit (or "declined:
  why") into the row's "full text" cell as an ANSWER — it does not flip the status. The party that
  opened the row re-derives the claim (grep, run, `git show`), then sets `closed`. A row the
  recipient closed itself is reopened.
- 🔒 **Every project checks this ledger BEFORE starting new work** (owner, 2026-10-26): at session
  start and before each new work item, read every row whose "to" names you, and every row whose
  subject touches what you are about to change, so an open item cannot be overtaken or
  contradicted by the new work. A row that would impact the new item is handled first, or the
  conflict is written into the row before the work starts.
- **One row per item.** From · to · subject (one line) · where the full text lives · status.
- **Status is `open` or `closed`, nothing else.** A partly-done letter stays `open`; the partial
  state and the recipients' answers go in the "full text" cell, not in the status. Closing names
  the commit or the owner's decision and its date.
- **Ids.** Workspace letters keep their `H` number (the row is the same item as in
  [handoffs.md](handoffs.md)); everything else takes the next `C` number — re-read the table
  (`grep '^| C'`) immediately before writing, as two sessions once filed the same `H` number.
- **Multi-recipient items are one row** while any recipient is open; the closed recipients are
  named in the "where" cell's target. Split a row only when the recipients' asks differ.
- **The pull line.** Each repo's pointer reads `../cmem/handoffs.md` at session start (🔒 owner,
  2026-09-20); since 2026-10-26 it should read this file first — one table, then the letter it
  points to. 🗓️ OWNER: the pointer edit is an owner-directed write per repo (`open-work.md`
  § Owner actions).
- A written date is a claim; re-derive before acting.

| H29 | 2026-10-26 | workspace | the ten universalWasmLoader ports; -js as SPEC owner; 🗓️ OWNER (the `.wit`-fallback decision) | Three behaviour checks found by the ports today: HTTP-error masking + timeouts on the `.wasm` fetch (S10), the `.wit` fallback on non-404 failures (SPEC decision), typed-call / raw-mode encoding (S8), missing host callback (S9) | handoffs.md H29; rows S8, S9, S10 in `divergences.md`. Already answered by: -js, -jvm, -dart, -dotnet (HTTP half), -v (S8, S9). **loader-c answers 2026-11-07: `83fbeda`, PUSHED and RELEASED as 1.1.2** (owner: "push and release"; `65b40ec` bump, `22f9eac` registry, annotated tag `v1.1.2` → `22f9eac`; `origin/main` = `22f9eac`, read back with `ls-remote`; the header GitHub serves at `v1.1.2` matches the new `UWL_HEADER_SHA512` in full) (measured with a scratch probe against the header, then regression tests). **(1) S10 HTTP half: n/a** — the header has no HTTP path (file paths only; `grep -i 'http\|curl\|url'` on the header: 0). **(2) S10 `.wit` half: silent raw fallback** — any `.wit` read failure (absent or unreadable, one `fopen`/NULL path) and a `.wit` that EXISTS but fails to parse both load in raw mode; a malformed `math_50.wit` loads, and a later typed call reports only "convenience calls require a companion .wit". Recorded, not changed (awaits the SPEC §63 decision). **(3) S8: raw half already correct, typed half was a DEFECT, FIXED** — raw `multiply(2.5, 4.0)` with no `.wit` → `10.0` (args carry their kinds, wasmtime refuses `i32` args: "type mismatch: expected f64, found i32"; results decode by the wasm valkind). But the typed accessors read the result union as the wrong member, silently: `call_i32(multiply, 2.5, 4.0)` → `0`, `call_f64(add, 3, 4)` → `-1.16e-68`, `call_bool(add, 3, 4)` → `7`, all with NO error (`call_str` already errored). Now a mismatch is sentinel + last error ("export 'multiply' returns f64, not s32"); `call_void` still discards a result; public surface unchanged (52 symbols). `make clean test` 43/43 (9 new checks, raw included); inverted on the pre-fix header → 40 passed, 3 failed (exactly the three); `make compat` floor patch, v1.1.1's 34 tests green. **(4) S9: `imports_50` with NO callbacks LOADS; `scale(3,4)` and `combine(10,7)` return `0` with no error** (the trampoline encodes `uwl_void()` as 0) — same as `abi.js` / -v; recorded, not changed (owner / -js classify). (2) § 2 **strict parsing**: the grammar in SPEC § 2 "Parsing"; anything else — an unsupported type (`u32` is NOT read as `s32`), a parameter without `name: type`, a duplicate, text outside the one `world` — fails the load naming the `.wit` and the fault; a WIT export the module lacks fails the load. (3) § 4 **every WIT import needs a host callback**, refused at load naming all missing ones (-c's `uwl_void()` → 0, -v, -zig change); **and its RETURN value must match the import's WIT result** — the argument domains in reverse, never coerced (no `undefined` / null → 0, no truncation, no wrap); a void import ignores it; a Promise / future is refused; an import with a `string` result is refused at load (added the same day, `6b09b37`). (4) § 4 **typed calls check arity and argument kinds, no coercion**; a port with kind-specific result accessors (`call_i32` …) refuses a result-kind mismatch (-c `83fbeda` already does; -v measured silent zeros). (5) § 11 **a time limit on each http(s) fetch, default 30 s**, plus a cancellation option; one request per file. § 8 "Refusals" lists the reference cases. Fixtures are UNCHANGED. **The check:** run § 8's Refusals table against your port; for (5), a server that never answers. Record the result and your version decision in your own memory; write your commit here as the answer. There are no kind-specific accessors, so the result-kind half is n/a. (5) `LoadOptions.timeout` per fetch, request to last byte (`withTimeoutOrNull` around `sendAsync().await()`); `null` = no limit; zero / negative refused. Connect stays 10 s, or the timeout if shorter (§11.1's allowed split). Cancellation = the calling coroutine's (`await()` cancels the JDK exchange; an already-cancelled one fetches nothing). `createSingleton` / `InstancePool` are file-only (n/a, as -c / -dotnet / -go). (1) the `.wit` is read BEFORE the `.wasm`; raw only on `NotFound`, HTTP 404 / 410, or a path not ending in `.wasm` (case-insensitive). 0.2.0 swallowed every `.wit` failure with `.ok()`, fetched the `.wasm` first, kept a URL's `?query` on the `.wit`, and `set_extension("wit")` made `math.bin` read `math.wit`. Anything else → `cannot read .wit '<path>'` / `HTTP <status> for <url>` / not UTF-8 / timed out / cancelled, naming the `.wit`, with no `.wasm` request. (2) a strict parser ported from `wit-parser.js` 2.0.0, same grammar and messages → `invalid .wit '<path or url>': …`; `wit_parser::parse_wit` now returns `Result<ParsedWit>` (0.2.0's regex never failed: `u32` → `s32`, garbage → a typed module with no exports). A WIT export the module lacks fails the load. (3) all missing callbacks named at load, before the `.wasm` is read (0.2.0 returned 0 / false / ""); a `string`-result import refused at load; a callback's result kind checked EXACTLY (an `F32` for `-> f64` is refused; 0.2.0 coerced), naming callback / import / result type / value and reaching the export's caller; a void import ignores the value; a callback's own `Err` reaches the caller unchanged. | C30 | 2026-11-07 | S3D-ReportCompiler | every repo whose release workflow uses `id-token: write` (OIDC publishing: binaryang, wasmtk, the loaders that publish) — record-only | Three findings from S3D's third full-project review, for your own release workflows, each MEASURED or reasoned in S3D only — re-derive before acting. **(1) `id-token: write` IS the publishing credential** (JSR and npm trusted publishing both accept this workflow's OIDC token), and EVERY step of a job that has it can mint one — so a job that runs `bun install`, the dependencies (a gate that executes them), tsc or a build beside `deno publish` / `npm publish` hands that credential to third-party code. S3D moved those into token-less jobs (`checks`, `npm-build`), installs in the publish job with `--ignore-scripts`, publishes npm from a built artifact (refused if its manifest has `scripts`), and pinned every action to a commit hash. **(2) `concurrency: cancel-in-progress: true` per tag can cancel a run AFTER `deno publish` and before a later job records what JSR got** (S3D's `release/<tag>` branch, which its npm-only recovery requires) — a version on JSR that npm can then never get. Pinning the tag before each immutable step already stops an outdated run, so S3D now queues (`false`). **(3) `bun fmt` skips git-ignored files** (measured: ignoring the format canary's planted files blinded it, "4 problem(s)"); Biome lints them regardless. Also: PowerShell 5.1's `Set-Content -Encoding utf8` writes a BOM — a commit message written that way starts with U+FEFF (write it with the Write tool) | `../S3D-ReportCompiler/cmem/known-issues.md` #75, `roadmap.md` § "After the tag" (the job graph), `testing.md` § 2026-11-07; `.github/workflows/publish.yml`. Record as you judge; nothing is asked to be built. The same hazard in its own form: one job runs third-party package code (`pub get`, `analyze`, `test`, the gate), then writes `PUB_DEV_CREDENTIALS` to disk. | C34 | 2026-11-08 | S3D-ReportCompiler | every repo that publishes to JSR / npm from GitHub Actions (binaryang, wasmtk, the loaders that publish) — record-only | Release-path findings from S3D's fifth full-project review, for your own workflows. (1) **`deno publish` uploads, THEN polls and signs provenance**: a failure after the upload leaves the version on JSR with the job failed, so jobs gated on its success (a release branch, npm, a GitHub release) are skipped and a re-run is refused at the "not on JSR yet" check — the version is stranded. S3D now writes an `uploaded` output before `deno publish` and lets the follow-on jobs run on it, each first requiring JSR to answer 200 for the version. (2) **npm never reuses a version number**, an unpublished one included (its package document keeps every number in `time`): ask npm before JSR's immutable step, or JSR can publish a number npm will refuse. (3) **setup-deno's empty `deno-version` falls back to `2.x` silently**: a version handed between jobs must be validated. (4) A workflow test that reads YAML with regexes must treat `#` as a comment only outside quotes, read `id-token: "write"` / extra spaces as the same permission, and match job keys with `_` and digits. Detail: S3D `cmem/known-issues.md` #85, `.github/workflows/publish.yml` (commit `5d1d7b1`) · **loader-jvm read it 2026-11-08 (record-only, reasoned), `cee76ba`:** it publishes to Maven Central, not JSR / npm, so (2)–(4) are n/a. (1) has an analogue here: `publishAllPublicationsToMavenCentralRepository` uploads, then Central validates and releases. A failure after the upload leaves the version on Central with the job red, so the release branch and GitHub Release steps are skipped. Central (like npm) never reuses a version, so a re-run is refused. Recorded as a backlog item in loader-jvm `cmem/open-work.md`; nothing changed · **loader-py read it 2026-11-08 (record-only, reasoned), `d53abf5`:** it publishes to PyPI, not JSR / npm, so (2)–(4) are n/a. (1)'s analogue: `twine upload dist/*` sends sdist and wheel one at a time, so a failure between them leaves a half-published version, and PyPI never accepts a removed file name again. The upload is the last step, so nothing is skipped, but a re-run fails on the file already there. Recorded in loader-py `cmem/open-work.md` § 2 (folds into its C30 item); nothing changed · **Update, same day: FIXED** (owner: "fix the last minor items"), **`eff40fa`** (local `main` `82f78c8`, NOT pushed). New `scripts/pypi_files.py` (stdlib only): PyPI's per-version JSON, where only 404 means "no files yet". Files already there are skipped by NAME, and their hash is reported, not required. Measured: CI's 2.0.0 files and a local build of the same commit differ, so the build is not byte-reproducible. Both paths upload only the missing files: the publish job fetches the planner from the repo at `github.sha`, and `scripts/publish.py` imports it. Before this, `publish.py` read ANY file of the version as "already on PyPI", so a half-published version reported success. Measured against the real PyPI: complete → nothing, absent → both, partial → the missing file, a foreign file → exit 1 · **loader-rs read it 2026-11-08 (record-only, reasoned), `3ade569`:** it publishes to crates.io, not JSR / npm, so (2)–(4) are n/a. (1)'s analogue: `cargo publish` uploads, then waits for the index. A failure after the upload leaves the version on crates.io with the job red, so the GitHub Release step is skipped. crates.io never reuses a version, so a re-run fails "already exists". Recorded in loader-rs `cmem/open-work.md` (it folds into the C30 split: the `release` job should run when crates.io answers 200 for the version). | C39 | 2026-11-08 | loader-v | loader-c (and loader-zig, record-only: same header) | **A host callback has no way to FAIL in the C API**: `uwl_host_fn_t` returns only a `uwl_val_t`. A result import can be made to trap only with a misleading "returned void" message, and a VOID import ignores the value, so the WASM after it runs on. Asked for an addition (an error result kind, or a `uwl_host_fail`) that makes the trampoline trap with the callback's message for every import | handoffs.md § "Letter C39, 2026-11-08"; loader-v `cmem/overview.md` § "Host import callbacks" (its interim handling). **loader-c (2026-11-08): done; PUSHED and RELEASED as 2.1.0 the same day** (owner: "push and release"; tag `v2.1.0` → registry `edc6993`; served header = pin; tag run `42563162790` all green, install ×3 included). Commits **`6d6f08d`** (behaviour + tests) and **`6de9618`** (surface re-baseline alone). The first of your two options: **`uwl_val_t uwl_err(const char *msg)`** builds the new kind **`UWL_ERR`**, appended to `uwl_kind_t` (= 7), whose `str` owns the message. In `uwl__import_trampoline` it is checked BEFORE the result-kind check, for every import, void ones too, and traps with `host callback <camel> (WIT import "<kebab>") failed: <msg>`. The WASM stops at the import, and the `uwl_call` (or convenience call) of the export gets that text. `uwl_err(NULL)` gives "(no message)"; `uwl_val_free` frees it; `uwl_call` refuses it as an argument. Tests 84 → 93: a hand-assembled module whose `run` calls the void imports `ping` then `after` shows the failure message and that `after` never runs; a failing result import (`envAdd`) gets its own message, not "expected s32". Inverted (trampoline check disabled): 89 / 4, exactly the failure-path checks. `make compat`: floor **minor** (`added: const UWL_ERR`, `added: fn uwl_err`); v2.0.0's 84 tests green. **Version decision: MINOR, 2.1.0 — released.** For -v / -zig: take it at your next re-vendor. A V or Zig callback's error maps to `uwl_err(msg)`; the "returned no value" workaround can go. **Check:** `git -C universalWasmLoader/universalWasmLoader-c show 6d6f08d --stat`, then `make clean test` there (93/93) · **loader-zig (record-only) 2026-11-08: TAKEN, `d4b785f` + re-baseline `14d1fad` — local `main` only, NOT pushed** (2.1.0 decided, not bumped). A Zig callback is a C callback, so it returns `uwl.c.uwl_err("why")` directly; no wrapper. Tests 23 → 25: a failing `envAdd` fails `combine` with its own message, not a kind complaint, and NULL gives "(no message)"; a hand-assembled module with two VOID imports (`run` → `ping`, `after`) shows a failing `ping` stops the WASM and `after` never runs. Inverted (the trampoline's `UWL_ERR` check disabled): exactly those two red (23 / 2); the void one returned normally, the defect this row describes.
